with Theorem
4.6
can naturally lead to one.
Instead of this we adopt another way.
Recall that Fadell–Rabinowitz bifurcation theorems
[
26
,
27
]

were generalized to the case of arbitrary compact Lie groups by
Bartsch and Clapp
[
3
]
, and Bartsch
[
2
]
.
Bartsch and Clapp
[
3
, §4]
proved the following theorem
(according to our notations).

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
Z
be a finite dimensional Hilbert space equipped with a linear isometric action of a compact Lie group
G

with
Z^{G}=\{0\}
, let
\delta>0
,
\epsilon>0
,
\lambda^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
and
for every
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]
let

f_{\lambda}:B_{Z}(0,\epsilon)\to\mathbb{R}
be a function of class
C^{2}
.
Assume that

(a)

the functions
\{(\lambda,u)\to f_{\lambda}(u)\}
and

\{(\lambda,u)\to f^{\prime}_{\lambda}(u)\}
are continuous on

[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]\times B_{Z}(0,\epsilon)
;

(b)

u=0
is a critical point of each
f_{\lambda}
,
L_{\lambda}:=D(\nabla f_{\lambda})(0)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(Z)
is an isomorphism
for each
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
, and
L_{\lambda^{\ast}}=0
;

(c)

the eigenspaces
Z_{\lambda}
and
Z_{\mu}
belonging to

\sigma(L_{\lambda})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}
and
\sigma(L_{\mu})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}
,
respectively, are isomorphic if
(\lambda-\lambda^{\ast})(\mu-\lambda^{\ast})>0
for any

\lambda,\mu\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
, and thus
the number

d:=\ell(SZ)-\min\{\ell(SZ_{\lambda})+\ell(SZ_{\mu}^{\bot}),\ell(SZ_{\lambda}^{\bot})+\ell(SZ_{\mu})\}

(5.12)

is independent of
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
and
\mu\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)
,
where
Z_{\lambda}^{\bot}
is the orthogonal complementary of
Z_{\lambda}
in
Z
and

\ell
denote the
(\mathscr{G},h^{\ast})
-length used in
[
3
]
.

Then one at least of the following assertions holds if
d>0
:

(i)

u=0
is not an isolated critical point of
f_{\lambda^{\ast}}
;

(ii)

there exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\mathbb{R}

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq d

and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
),

f_{\lambda}
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct critical

G
-orbits different from
0
, which converge to

0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

In particular,
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]\times B_{Z}(0,\epsilon)

is a bifurcation point of
f^{\prime}_{\lambda}(u)=0
.

Theorem 5.12
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.6

suppose that a compact Lie group
G
acts on
H
orthogonally, which induces
C^{1}
isometric actions on
X
,
and that both
U
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
are
G
-invariant (and hence
H^{0}_{\lambda}
,
H^{\pm}_{\lambda}

are
G
-invariant subspaces).
If the fixed point set of the induced
G
-action on
H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is
\{0\}
then
one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not an isolated solution in
\{\lambda^{\ast}\}\times U^{X}
of the equation (
4.27
);

(ii)

there exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\mathbb{R}

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\ell(SH^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}})

and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
),

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct critical

G
-orbits different from
0
, which converge to

0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

In particular,
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]\times U^{X}

is a bifurcation point of (
4.27
).

Proof.

Follow the notations in the proof of Theorem
4.6
. In the present situation,
for each
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]
,
the maps
\psi(\lambda,\cdot)
and
\Phi_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)
are

G
-equivariant, and
C^{2}
functional
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
given by (
4.30
) is
G
-invariant.
As in the proof of Theorem
4.2
we obtain either

\displaystyle 0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\hbox{is a strict local}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox{minimizer of}\;\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}),\\
\hbox{maximizer of}\;\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]\end{array}\right.

or

\displaystyle 0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\hbox{is a strict local}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox{maximizer of}\;\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}),\\
\hbox{minimizer of}\;\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta].\end{array}\right.

Note that for each
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is a nondegenerate critical point of
the functional
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
by Remark
A.4
(ii).
Let
L_{\lambda}:=D(\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ})(0)
. Then
L_{\lambda^{\ast}}=0
by
(
A.7
). Hence this and (
A.5
)-(
A.6
) show that functionals

f_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
on
B_{Z}(0,\epsilon)
with
Z:=H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
,

\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]
,
satisfy the conditions (a)-(b) in Theorem
5.11
.
(In fact,
\lambda\mapsto L_{\lambda}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(Z)
is also continuous by (
A.7
).)

It remains to prove that they also satisfy the condition (c) in Theorem
5.11
.

Firstly, we assume (
5.1
) holding. Then it implies that

L_{\lambda}
is positive definite for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
,
and negative definite for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)
.
It follows that
\sigma(L_{\lambda})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}=\emptyset
and so
Z_{\lambda}=\emptyset

for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
, and

\sigma(L_{\lambda})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}=\sigma(L_{\lambda})
and so
Z_{\lambda}=Z

for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)
.
Hence the condition (c) in Theorem
5.11
is satisfied. Note also that in
the present situation we have

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell(SZ)-\min\{\ell(SZ_{\lambda})+\ell(SZ_{\mu}^{\bot}),\ell(SZ_{\lambda}^{\bot})+\ell(SZ_{\mu})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell(SZ)-\min\{0,\ell(SZ)+\ell(SZ)\}=\ell(SZ)

for any of
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
and
\mu\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)

because the
(\mathscr{G},h^{\ast})
-length of a
G
-space,
\ell(X)
, is equal to zero if and only if
X=\emptyset
by

[
3
, Proposition 1.5]
.

Next, let (
5.1
) be satisfied. Then we have

\sigma(L_{\lambda})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}=\sigma(L_{\lambda})
and so
Z_{\lambda}=Z

for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
, and

\sigma(L_{\lambda})\cap\mathbb{R}^{-}=\emptyset
and so
Z_{\lambda}=\emptyset

for any
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)
.
These lead to

\displaystyle d=\ell(SZ)-\min\{\ell(SZ_{\lambda})+\ell(SZ_{\mu}^{\bot}),\ell(SZ_{\lambda}^{\bot})+\ell(SZ_{\mu})\}=\ell(SZ)

for any of
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast})
and
\mu\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\delta)
.

Note that we have always
\ell(SZ)>0
by

[
3
, Proposition 1.5]
. Hence Theorem
5.11
gives the desired conclusions.
∎

When
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{-id_{H},id_{H}\}
(resp.
G=S^{1}
)
we have
d=\dim H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
(resp.
d=\frac{1}{2}\dim H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
) as showed in
Remark
5.14
below.

Corollary 5.13
.

Under the assumptions of one of Corollaries
4.7
,
4.8

suppose that
H
is equipped with an orthogonal action of a compact Lie group
G

which induces a
C^{1}
isometric action on
X
, and that
U
,
\mathcal{L}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
are
G
-invariant.
Then the conclusions of Theorem
5.12
hold true.

Remark 5.14
.

For a finite-dimensional real
G
-module
M
with
M^{G}=\{0\}
and
h^{\ast}=H^{\ast}_{G}
Borel cohomology it was proved in
[
3
, Propositions 2.4,2.6]
:

(i)

if
G=(\mathbb{Z}/p)^{r}
, where
r>0
and
p
is a prime, then
(\mathscr{G},H^{\ast}_{G})
-length(SM) is equal
to
\dim M
for
p=2
, and to
\frac{1}{2}\dim M
for
p>2
;

(ii)

if
G=(S^{1})^{r}
,
r>0
, then
(\mathscr{G},H^{\ast}_{G})
-length(SM) is equal
to
\frac{1}{2}\dim M
;

(iii)

if
G=S^{1}\times\Gamma
,
\Gamma
is finite, and such that the fixed point set of
S^{1}\equiv S^{1}\times\{e\}

is trivial, then
(\mathscr{G},H^{\ast}_{G})
-length(SM) is equal
to
\frac{1}{2}\dim M
.

Here the coefficients in the Borel cohomology
H^{\ast}_{G}
are
G=(\mathbb{Z}/p)^{r}
in (i), and
\mathbb{Q}
in (ii) and (iii).

Let us point out that it is possible to generalize the above results with methods in
[
2
, §7.5]
.

As remarked below Corollary
4.8
, Theorem
5.12

and Corollary
5.13
are powerful to applications in Lagrange systems and geodesics on Finsler manifolds.

Remark 5.15
.

Under the assumptions of any one of Theorems
4.11
,
4.10

and Corollaries
4.12
,
4.13
,
if
H
is equipped with an orthogonal action of a compact Lie group
G

such that
U
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
are
G
-invariant and that
the fixed point set of the induced
G
-action on
H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is
\{0\}
,
then the corresponding conclusions with Theorem
5.9
(for
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{{\rm id},-{\rm id}\}
or
S^{1}
)
and those with Theorem
5.12
with
X
replaced by
H
hold true.
Compare the latter with
[
3
, Therrem 3.1]
by Bartsch and Clapp.

5.2
Bifurcations starting a nontrivial critical orbit

Hypothesis 5.16

(
[
45
, Hypothesis 2.20]
)
.

(i)
Let
G
be a compact
Lie group, and
\mathcal{H}
a
C^{3}
Hilbert-Riemannian
G
-space
(that is,
{\mathcal{H}}
is a
C^{3}

G
-Hilbert manifold with a Riemannian
metric
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
such that
T{\mathcal{H}}
is a
C^{2}
Riemannian
G
-vector bundle (see
[
74
]
)).

(ii)
The
C^{1}
functional
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}
is
G
-invariant, the gradient

\nabla\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{H}\to T\mathcal{H}
is Gâteaux differentiable
(i.e., under any
C^{3}
local chart the functional
\mathcal{L}

has a Gâteaux differentiable gradient map), and
\mathcal{O}
is an isolated
critical orbit which is a
C^{3}
critical submanifold with Morse index
\mu_{\mathcal{O}}
.

Under the above assumptions
let
\pi:N\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{O}
denote the normal bundle of it. The
bundle is a
C^{2}
-Hilbert vector bundle over
\mathcal{O}
, and can
be considered as a subbundle of
T_{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{H}}
via the
Riemannian metric
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
. The metric
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)

induces a natural
C^{2}
orthogonal bundle
projection
{\bf\Pi}:T_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{H}\to N\mathcal{O}
. For
\varepsilon>0
,
the so-called normal disk bundle of radius
\varepsilon
is denoted by

N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon):=\{(x,v)\in N\mathcal{O}\,|\,\|v\|_{x}<\varepsilon\}
.
If
\varepsilon>0
is small enough the exponential map
\exp
gives a
C^{2}
-diffeomorphism

\digamma
from
N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
onto an open
neighborhood of
\mathcal{O}
in
{\mathcal{H}}
,
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{O},\varepsilon)
.
For
x\in\mathcal{O}
, let
\mathscr{L}_{s}(N\mathcal{O}_{x})
denote the space
of those operators
S\in\mathscr{L}(N\mathcal{O}_{x})
which are self-adjoint
with respect to the inner product
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)_{x}
, i.e.

(\!(S_{x}u,v)\!)_{x}=(\!(u,S_{x}v)\!)_{x}
for all
u,v\in N\mathcal{O}_{x}
. Then we have a
C^{1}
vector bundle
\mathscr{L}_{s}(N\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{O}
whose fiber at
x\in\mathcal{O}
is given by

\mathscr{L}_{s}(N\mathcal{O}_{x})
.

Hypothesis 5.17
.

Under Hypothesis
5.16
, let for some
x_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
the pair

(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}})

satisfy the corresponding conditions with Hypothesis
1.1
with
X=H=N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}
.
(For this goal we only need require that the pair
(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp_{x_{0}},B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon))

satisfy the corresponding conditions with Hypothesis
1.1
with
X=H=T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}

by
[
45
, Lemma 2.8]
.) Let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in C^{1}(\mathcal{H},\mathbb{R})
be
G
-invariant, have
a critical orbit
\mathcal{O}
, and also satisfy:

(i)

the gradient
\nabla(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})
is Gâteaux differentiable, and its derivative
at any
u\in B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)
,

d^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})(u)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}),

is also a compact linear operator.

(ii)

B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}),\;u\mapsto d^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})(u)

is continuous at
0\in T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}
.
(Thus the assumptions on
\mathcal{G}
assure that the functionals

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{L}-\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
,

\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, also satisfy the conditions of

[
45
, Theorems 2.21 and 2.22]
.)

Under Hypothesis
5.17
, we say
\mathcal{O}
to be a
bifurcation
G
-orbit
with parameter
\lambda^{\ast}
of the equation

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(u)=\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u),\quad u\in\mathcal{H}

(5.19)

if for any
\varepsilon>0
and for any neighborhood
\mathscr{U}
of
\mathcal{O}
in
\mathcal{H}

there exists a solution
G
-orbit
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\neq\mathcal{O}
in
\mathscr{U}
of
(
5.19
) with some
\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast}-\varepsilon,\lambda^{\ast}+\varepsilon)
.
Equivalently, for some (and so any) fixed
x_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{n},u_{n})\subset(\lambda^{\ast}-\varepsilon,\lambda^{\ast}+\varepsilon)\times\mathcal{H}
such that

(\lambda_{n},u_{n})\to(\lambda^{\ast},x_{0}),\quad\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(u_{n})=\lambda_{n}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(u_{n})\quad\hbox{and}\quad u_{n}\notin\mathcal{O}\quad\forall n.

(5.20)

For any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}:=\exp_{x_{0}}({N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}})

is a
C^{2}

slice
for the action of
G
on
\mathcal{H}

in the following sense:

(i)

\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
is a
C^{2}
submanifold of
\mathcal{H}
containing
x_{0}
, and the tangent space

T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}\subset T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}
is a closed complement to
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{O}
;

(ii)

G\cdot\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
is a neighborhood of the orbit
\mathcal{O}=G\cdot x_{0}
, i.e., the orbit of every

x\in\mathcal{H}
sufficiently close to
x_{0}
must intersect
\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
and
(G\cdot x)\pitchfork\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
;

(iii)

\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
is invariant under the isotropy group
G_{x_{0}}
.

(The
C^{1}

G
-action is sufficient for (ii).)
Clearly, a point
u\in{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}}
near
0_{x_{0}}\in{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}}

is a critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}}

if and only if
x:=\exp_{x_{0}}(u)
is a critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}}
.
Since
d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)[\xi]=0\;\forall\xi\in T_{x}(G\cdot x)
and

T_{x}\mathcal{H}=T_{x}(G\cdot x)\oplus T_{x}\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}
, we deduce

d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad d(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}})(u)=0.

(5.21)

Note that for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
the orthogonal complementary of
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{O}
in
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}
,
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}
,
is an invariant subspace of

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}(x_{0}):=d^{2}(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})(0)\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}

(in particular
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}):=d^{2}(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})(0)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}):=d^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{B_{T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{H}}(0,\varepsilon)})(0)
)
.
Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}(x_{0})^{\bot}
(resp.
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
) denote
the restriction self-adjoint operator of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}(x_{0})
(resp.
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})
,
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})
)
from
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}
to itself. Then
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}(x_{0})^{\bot}=d^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}})(0)
and

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}=d^{2}(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}})(0),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}=d^{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}})(0).

Applying Corollary
4.3
to

(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}})
and using (
5.21
) we obtain:

Theorem 5.18
.

Under Hypothesis
5.17
,
suppose that
\lambda^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
an isolated eigenvalue of

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}v-\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}v=0,\quad v\in N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}},

(5.22)

and that
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
is either semi-positive or semi-negative.
Then
\mathcal{O}
is a bifurcation
G
-orbit
with parameter
\lambda^{\ast}
of the equation
(
5.19
)
and one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

\mathcal{O}
is not an isolated critical orbit of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
;

(ii)

for every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
near
\lambda^{\ast}
there is a critical point

u_{\lambda}\notin\mathcal{O}
of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
converging to
x_{0}
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
;

(iii)

there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda
of
\lambda^{\ast}
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
has at least two critical points which sit in
\mathcal{S}_{x_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{O}
converging to

x_{0}
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
is invertible, or equivalently

{\rm Ker}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}))=T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{O}
. Then

0
is not an eigenvalue of (
5.22
)
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}

is an eigenvalue of (
5.22
) if and only if
1/\lambda

is an eigenvalue of compact linear self-adjoint operator

L_{x_{0}}:=[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}]^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}).

Hence
\sigma(L_{x_{0}})\setminus\{0\}=\{1/\lambda_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{R}

with
|\lambda_{k}|\to\infty
, and each
1/\lambda_{k}
has finite multiplicity.
Let
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{k}
be the eigenspace corresponding to
1/\lambda_{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then

\displaystyle N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{0}:={\rm Ker}(L_{x_{0}})={\rm Ker}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}),

\displaystyle N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{k}={\rm Ker}(I/\lambda_{k}-L_{x_{0}})={\rm Ker}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}-\lambda_{k}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}),\;\forall k\in\mathbb{N},

(5.23)

and
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}=\oplus^{\infty}_{k=0}N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{k}
.
Since
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{O}\subset{\rm Ker}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}))\cap{\rm Ker}(\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0}))
,
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{O}
is a nondegenerate critical orbit
of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
if and only if
\lambda
is not an eigenvalue of (
5.22
).
Applying Corollary
4.4
to

(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}})
and using (
5.21
) we obtain:

Theorem 5.19
.

Under Hypothesis
5.17
,
suppose that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
is invertible
and that
\lambda^{\ast}=\lambda_{k_{0}}
is an eigenvalue of (
5.22
) as above.
Then the conclusions of Theorem
5.18
hold true if
one of the following two conditions is also satisfied:

(a)

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
is positive;

(b)

each
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{k}
in (
5.2
) with
L=[\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(0)]^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\prime\prime}(0)

is an invariant subspace of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
(e.g. these are true if
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}

commutes with
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}
), and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(x_{0})^{\bot}

is either positive definite or negative one on
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{k_{0}}
.

Of course, corresponding to Theorem
4.2
there is a more general result.
Moreover, if either
G_{x_{0}}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
acts on
N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}
via the antipodal map
or
G_{x_{0}}=S^{1}
acts orthogonally on
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}
and
the fixed point set of the induced
S^{1}
-action on
N\mathcal{O}_{x_{0}}^{0}
is
\{0\}
,
we may apply Theorem
5.9
to

(\mathcal{L}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\circ\exp|_{N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}},N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}})

near
0=0_{x_{0}}\in N\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)_{x_{0}}
to get a stronger result.

We can also apply to Corollaries
4.7
,
4.8
,
Theorem
5.12
and Corollary
5.13

to suitably chosen slices in the setting of
[
43
]
to get some corresponding results.
It is better for us using this idea in concrete applications.
For Theorems
5.18
,
5.19
there also exists a remark similar to Remark
5.15
.

In applications, for example, to closed geodesics on Finsler or Riemannian manifolds,
Hypothesis
5.16
cannot be satisfied since the natural
S^{1}
action on Hilbert manifold

H^{1}(S^{1},M)
is only continuous. However, we can still use the above ideas to complete our aims.

6
Bifurcations for potential operators of Banach-Hilbert regular functionals

The aim of this section is to generalize partial results in last three sections to
potential operators of Banach-Hilbert regular functionals on Banach spaces.
Different from bifurcation results in previous sections, these bifurcation theorems
are on Banach (rather than Hilbert) spaces.
There exist a few splitting theorems for Banach-Hilbert regular functionals
in the literature (though they were expressed with different terminologies),
for example,
[
8
, Theorem 1.2]
,
[
33
, Theorem 2.5]
and
[
23
, page 436, Theorem 1]

(and
[
71
, Theorem 1.4]

for the special nondegenerate case).
See Remarks
B.1
,
B.7
for some comments and relations among them.
We here choose the setting of the parameterized versions of

[
8
, Theorem 1.2]
given in Appendix
B
.
Actually, it is not hard to deduce a parameterized version
of
[
33
, Theorem 2.5]
after the corresponding form of nondegenerate case of it
is proved by slightly more troublesome arguments.

In this section we always assume that
H
is a Hilbert space with inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{H}

and the induced norm
\|\cdot\|
,
X
is a Banach space with
norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
, and that they satisfy the condition (S) in Appendix
B
, i.e.,

X\subset H
is dense in
H
and the inclusion
X\hookrightarrow H
is continuous.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
H
and
X
be as above,
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{R}

an open interval containing
\lambda^{\ast}
. For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathbb{R}
be a
C^{2}
-functional having critical point
0\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
and satisfying
for some constant
M_{\lambda}>0
:

(1)

|d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u]|\leq M_{\lambda}\|u\|,\;\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
\forall u\in X
.

(2)

|d^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u,v]|\leq M_{\lambda}\|u\|\cdot\|v\|,\;\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
\forall u,v\in X
; and therefore
there exists a map
B_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
defined by

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u,v]=(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H},\quad\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta),\;\forall u,v\in X.

(3)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
there is a
C^{2}
map
A_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X
such that

d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u]=(A_{\lambda}(x),u)_{H}
for all
(x,u)\in B_{X}(0,\delta)\times X
.
(That is, the condition (c’) in Theorem
B.3
holds.)

Suppose also that the following conditions are satisfied:

(a)

The condition (sd’) (or (f1) and (sf2)-(sf3)) in Theorem
B.8
is satisfied.
(These naturally hold if
\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto A(\lambda,u):=A_{\lambda}(u)\in X
is
C^{3}
.)

(b)

\sigma(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X})\setminus\{0\}

is bounded away from the imaginary axis, and
H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}:={\rm Ker}({B}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))

is a nontrivial subspace of finite dimension contained in
X
. (Thus
0<\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}<\infty
.)

(c)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

\sigma(B_{\lambda}(0)|_{X})
is bounded away from the imaginary axis,
and
H^{0}_{\lambda}:={\rm Ker}({B}_{\lambda}(0))=\{0\}
.

(d)

The negative definite space
H^{-}_{\lambda}

of each
{B}_{\lambda}(0)
is of finite dimension (and hence is contained in
X
by Lemma
B.13
).

(e)

The Morse indexes of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}

at
0\in H
take, respectively, values
\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}
and
\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}+\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}

as
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
varies in
two deleted half neighborhoods of
\lambda^{\ast}
,
where
\mu_{\lambda}
and
\nu_{\lambda}
are the Morse index and the nullity of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}

at
0
(as defined below Lemma
B.2
), respectively.

Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

0\in X
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
there is a nonzero critical point

x_{\lambda}
of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
converging to
0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

(iii)

There is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda
of
\lambda^{\ast}
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
has at least two nonzero critical points converging to
zero as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

In particular,
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\mathbb{R}\times X
is a bifurcation point for the equation

d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(0)=0,\;x\in B_{X}(0,\delta).

(6.1)

Conversely, suppose that the above parameter space
\Lambda
is a metric space
and that the corresponding assumptions (1)-(3), the first condition in (b), and (d’) in Theorem
B.3
are satisfied.
Then
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
provided that
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times X
is a bifurcation point of (
6.1
).

Proof.

Step 1
(
Prove the first claim
).
Since either
\sigma(B_{\lambda}(0)|_{X})
or
\sigma(B_{\lambda}(0)|_{X})\setminus\{0\}
is bounded away from the imaginary axis,
there exists a direct sum decomposition of Banach spaces
X=X_{0}^{\lambda}\oplus X_{+}^{\lambda}\oplus X_{-}^{\lambda}
,
which corresponds to the spectral sets
\{0\}
,
\sigma_{+}(B_{\lambda}(0)|_{X})

and
\sigma_{-}(B_{\lambda}(0)|_{X})
. Denote by
P_{0}^{\lambda}
,
P_{+}^{\lambda}
and
P_{-}^{\lambda}
the corresponding
projections to this decomposition.

Let
H^{+}_{\lambda}
,
H^{-}_{\lambda}
and
H^{0}_{\lambda}
be the positive definite, negative definite and zero spaces of

{B}_{\lambda}(0)
. Denote by
P^{\ast}_{\lambda}
the orthogonal projections onto
H^{\ast}_{\lambda}
,

\ast=+,-,0
.

By assumptions (b)-(d) and Lemma
B.13
, we have:

1)

X_{0}^{\lambda^{\ast}}=H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
,
X_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}=H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

and
X_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}
is a dense subspace in
H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.

2)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

X_{0}^{\lambda}=H^{0}_{\lambda}=\{0\}
,
X_{-}^{\lambda}=H^{-}_{\lambda}

and
X_{+}^{\lambda}
is a dense subspace in
H^{+}_{\lambda}
.

Hence the nullity
\nu_{\lambda}:=\dim X^{\lambda}_{0}
and Morse index
\mu_{\lambda}:=\dim X^{\lambda}_{-}

of the functional
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
at
0
are equal to
\dim H_{\lambda}^{0}
and

\dim H^{-}_{\lambda}
(that is, the nullity and Morse index of the quadratic form
({B}_{\lambda}(0)u,u)_{H}
on
H
),
respectively, and all of them are finite numbers.

By Theorem
B.8
there exist small numbers
0<\epsilon<\delta
and
\rho>0
with

[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\subset\Lambda
,
a (unique)
C^{1}
map

\mathfrak{h}:[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}\to X_{0}^{\lambda^{\ast}}\oplus H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}(\lambda,0)=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad(id_{X}-P_{0}^{\lambda^{\ast}})A_{\lambda}(z+\mathfrak{h}(\lambda,z))=0

(6.2)

for all
(\lambda,z)\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}
,
and another
C^{1}
map

[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\to X,\;({\lambda},x)\mapsto\Phi_{{\lambda}}(x)

such that

\Phi_{\lambda}
is a
C^{1}
origin-preserving diffeomorphism from

B_{X}(0,\epsilon)
onto an open neighborhood of
0
in
X
containing
B_{X}(0,\epsilon/4)
and that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}\circ\Phi_{\lambda}(x)=\|P^{\lambda^{\ast}}_{+}x\|_{H}^{2}-\|P^{\lambda^{\ast}}_{-}x\|_{H}^{2}+\mathscr{L}_{{\lambda}}(P^{\lambda^{\ast}}_{0}x+\mathfrak{h}({\lambda},P^{\lambda^{\ast}}_{0}x)),\,\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon).

(6.3)

Moreover, the functional

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}:B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}\to\mathbb{R},\;z\mapsto\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(z+\mathfrak{h}({\lambda},z))

is of class
C^{2}
, has the first-order derivative at
z_{0}\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}
,

\displaystyle d\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}(z_{0})[z]=\bigl(A_{\lambda}(z_{0}+\mathfrak{h}(\lambda,z_{0})),z\bigr)_{H},\quad\forall z\in H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0},

and the map
[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}\to\mathbb{R}\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(u)\in\mathbb{R}
is
C^{1}
.
Since the map
z\mapsto z+\mathfrak{h}({\lambda},z)

induces an one-to-one correspondence
between the critical points of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
near
0\in H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}

and those of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
near
0\in X
,
0\in H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}

is an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
if and only if

0\in X
is such a critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}

(after shrinking
\rho>0
if necessary).
By the assumption (c) and Claim
B.11
we get

Claim 6.2
.

For each
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,

0\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\cap H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}
is a nondegenerate critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
.

(Note that we have not used the condition (e)!)
By assumptions (b)-(e) we obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{\lambda}=0,\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\},

(6.4)

\displaystyle\hbox{either}\;\mu_{\lambda}=\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast},\;\hbox{and}\;\mu_{\lambda}=\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}+\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\hbox{or}\;\mu_{\lambda}=\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}+\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast},\;\hbox{and}\;\mu_{\lambda}=\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast}.

(6.6)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
, (
6.4
) implies that

0\in X
is a nondegenerate (and hence isolated) critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
.
It follows from this and Theorem
B.3
that

C_{q}(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=\delta_{q\mu_{\lambda}}{\bf K}\quad\forall q=0,1,\cdots.

(6.7)

By Claim
6.2
, (
6.3
) and Corollary
B.10
we obtain that for any Abel group
{\bf K}
,

C_{q}(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{q-\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})\quad\forall q=0,1,\cdots.

(6.8)

Then (
6.7
) and (
6.8
) lead to

\displaystyle C_{j}(\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta_{j0}{\bf K},\quad\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast},\\
\delta_{j\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}{\bf K},\quad\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast}\end{array}\right.

for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
if (
6.5
) holds, and

\displaystyle C_{j}(\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta_{j0}{\bf K},\quad\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast},\\
\delta_{j\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}{\bf K},\quad\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast}\end{array}\right.

for any
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
if (
6.6
) holds. These two groups of equalities and (
4.14
) lead, respectively, to

\displaystyle 0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\hbox{is a strict local}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox{minimizer of}\;\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast},\\
\hbox{maximizer of}\;\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast}\end{array}\right.

if (
6.5
) holds, and

\displaystyle 0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\;\hbox{is a strict local}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox{maximizer of}\;\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda<\lambda^{\ast},\\
\hbox{minimizer of}\;\mathscr{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},&\quad\forall\lambda>\lambda^{\ast}\end{array}\right.

if (
6.6
) holds. The desired conclusions follow from
Theorem
4.1
directly.

Step 2
(
Prove the second claim
).
Under the assumptions of the converse part, we have sequences

(\lambda_{n})\subset\Lambda
and
(x_{n})\subset X\setminus\{0\}
such that

\lambda_{n}\to\lambda^{\ast}
,
x_{n}\to 0
and
d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Now by contradiction let
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}=0
.
Then we can use Theorem
B.3
to get a neighborhood
\Lambda_{0}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\Lambda
,

\epsilon\in(0,\delta)
and a
C^{0}
map
\Lambda_{0}\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\varphi_{\lambda}(u)\in X
such that
each
\varphi_{\lambda}
is an origin-preserving
C^{1}
diffeomorphism
from
B_{X}(0,\epsilon)
onto an open neighborhood of zero in
X
containing
B_{X}(0,\epsilon/4)

and satisfies (
B.2
). Clearly, we can assume that
(\lambda_{n},x_{n})\in\Lambda_{0}\times B_{X}(0,\epsilon/4)

for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Note that
y_{n}:=(\varphi_{\lambda_{n}})^{-1}(x_{n})\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon)\setminus\{0\}

is a critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda_{n}}
for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. By (
B.2
) we deduce

0=d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})\circ d\varphi_{\lambda_{n}}(y_{n})=d(\mathscr{L}_{\lambda_{n}}\circ\varphi_{\lambda_{n}})(y_{n})=B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)y_{n}

and therefore
y_{n}=0
and
x_{n}=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. It contradicts the choices of
(x_{n})
.
(
Note
: It seems that the claim cannot be proved with the implicit function theorem because (d’) in Theorem
B.3

does not imply the continuity of the map
(\lambda,u)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(u)
near
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times X
.
Of course, if the condition (d’) is replaced by the first two in (sd’) we can directly use the implicit function theorem
to get the conclusion.)
∎

Now, we give analogues of Corollaries
4.3
and
4.4
and
5.10
under the following hypothesis.
(Related notations may be found in Appendix
B
. )

Hypothesis 6.3
.

Let
H
and
X
be as in Theorem
6.1
, and
\mathscr{L},\widehat{\mathscr{L}}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathbb{R}
two
C^{2}
functionals
having critical point
0\in X
and satisfying the conditions (a) and (b) above (
B.1
). Denote by
B
and
\widehat{B}
the
corresponding operators defined as in (
B.1
). Suppose that there exist
C^{2}
maps
A,\widehat{A}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X

verifying

d\mathscr{L}(x)[u]=(A(x),u)_{H}\quad\hbox{and}\quad d\widehat{\mathscr{L}}(x)[u]=(\widehat{A}(x),u)_{H}

for all
(x,u)\in B_{X}(0,\delta)\times X
and that each of
A^{\prime\prime}
and
\widehat{A}^{\prime\prime}
is either
C^{1}
or uniformly continuous
in some neighborhood of any line segment in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
.
Assume that
\lambda^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
is an isolated eigenvalue of

B(0)v-\lambda\widehat{B}(0)v=0,\quad v\in H,

(6.21)

and that for each
\lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
the operator
\mathfrak{B}_{\lambda}:=B(0)-\lambda\widehat{B}(0)

satisfies the following properties:

(i)

Either
\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{\lambda}|_{X})
or
\sigma(\mathfrak{B}_{\lambda}|_{X})\setminus\{0\}
is bounded away from the imaginary axis.

(ii)

H^{0}_{\lambda}:={\rm Ker}(\mathfrak{B}_{\lambda})\subset X
, and the negative definite space

H^{-}_{\lambda}
of
\mathfrak{B}_{\lambda}
is of finite dimension (and hence is contained in
X
by Lemma
B.13
).

Consider the bifurcation problem
near
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\mathbb{R}\times X
for the equation

d\mathscr{L}(u)=\lambda d\widehat{\mathscr{L}}(u),\quad u\in B_{X}(0,\delta).

(6.22)

The conditions (1)-(3) and (a)-(b) in Theorem
6.1

assure that Theorem
B.8
can be used in the proof of Theorem
6.1
.
Under Hypothesis
6.3
Theorem
B.8
is still true by Remark
B.9
.

Corollary 6.4
.

Under Hypothesis
6.3
, suppose:

(a)

{B}(0)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
has a decomposition
P(0)+Q(0)
, where
P(0)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is positive definite and
Q(0)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact.

(b)

\widehat{B}(0)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact, and either semi-positive or semi-negative.

Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not an isolated solution in
\{\lambda^{\ast}\}\times B_{X}(0,\delta)
of the equation (
6.22
).

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
near
\lambda^{\ast}
there is a nontrivial solution
u_{\lambda}
of (
6.22
) in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
which converges to
0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

(iii)

There is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda
of
\lambda^{\ast}
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
, (
6.22
) has at least two nontrivial solutions in

B_{X}(0,\delta)
, which converge to
0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

In particular,
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\mathbb{R}\times X
is a bifurcation point for (
6.22
).

Proof.

Let
m_{\lambda}
and
n_{\lambda}
denote the Morse index and the nullity of
the
C^{\infty}
functional
H\ni u\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{\lambda}(u):=([B(0)-\lambda\widehat{B}(0)]u,u)_{H}

at
0\in H
. Since
\lambda^{\ast}
is an isolated eigenvalue of (
6.21
),
we may take a number
\rho>0
so small that

n_{\lambda}=0
for each
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
.
Using (a) and the first condition in (b) we derive from Lemma
B.12
that
n_{\lambda^{\ast}}<\infty
.
By (i) and (ii) in Hypothesis
6.3
we can shrink
\rho>0
such that

m_{\lambda}=\mu_{\lambda}<\infty\quad\hbox{and}\quad n_{\lambda}=\nu_{\lambda}<\infty,\quad\forall\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho].

(6.23)

Now all conditions of Theorem
6.1
, except (e), are satisfied for
family
\{\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}=\mathscr{L}_{1}-\lambda\mathscr{L}_{2}\,|\,\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\}
.

By (a) and (b),
B(0)-\lambda\widehat{B}(0)=P(0)+(Q(0)-\lambda\widehat{B}(0))
,
where
(Q(0)-\lambda\widehat{B}(0))
is compact. It follows that the
family
\{\mathfrak{L}_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\}

satisfies the (PS) condition on any closed ball.
As in the proof of Corollary
4.3
, we can replace

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(0)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(0)
by

B(0)
and
\widehat{B}(0)
, respectively, to obtain

m_{\lambda}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}m_{\lambda^{\ast}},&\quad\forall\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast}),\\
m_{\lambda^{\ast}}+n_{\lambda^{\ast}},&\quad\forall\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\rho]\end{array}\right.

(6.24)

if
\widehat{B}(0)\geq 0
, and

m_{\lambda}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}m_{\lambda^{\ast}},&\quad\forall\lambda\in(\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{\ast}+\rho],\\
m_{\lambda^{\ast}}+n_{\lambda^{\ast}},&\quad\forall\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\rho,\lambda^{\ast})\end{array}\right.

(6.25)

if
\widehat{B}(0)\leq 0
. Hence (e) of Theorem
6.1
follows from (
6.23
) and (
6.24
)-(
6.25
).
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

Under Hypothesis
6.3
, suppose that the following two conditions are satisfied:

(a)

B(0)
is invertible, and
\widehat{B}(0)
is compact.

(b)

B(0)\widehat{B}(0)=\widehat{B}(0)B(0)
, and
B(0)

is either positive or negative on
{\rm Ker}(B(0)-\lambda^{\ast}\widehat{B}(0))
.

Then the conclusions of Corollary
6.4
hold true.
Moreover, if
B(0)
is positive definite, the condition (b) is unnecessary.

Proof.

The first paragraph in the proof of Corollary
6.4
is still valid
after changing “Using (a) and the first condition in (b)” into “Using (a)”.
Next, for the part
“
Let us choose
\delta>0
……, we get
either
(
4
) or (
4
).”
in the proof of Corollary
4.4
, replacing

\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(0)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(0)
by
B(0)
and
\widehat{B}(0)
, respectively,
we obtain that either (
6.24
) or (
6.25
) holds.
Then for
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}:=\mathscr{L}-\lambda\widehat{\mathscr{L}}
,
as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
we may use (
6.24
)-(
6.25
) and (
6.23
)
to derive the corresponding results with (
4
) and (
4
),
(
6
) and (
6
).
∎

By Theorem
5.1
and the proof of Theorem
6.1

we may directly get corresponding results with Theorem
5.9
and Corollary
5.10
.
Instead of these, as Theorem
5.12
and Corollary
5.13
we use Theorem
5.11
by Bartsch and Clapp
[
3
, §4]

to obtain:

Theorem 6.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
,
let
G
be a compact Lie group acting on
H
orthogonally,
which induces a
C^{1}
isometric action on
X
. Suppose that each
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
is
G
-invariant and that

A_{\lambda},B_{\lambda}
are equivariant, and that

H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}:={\rm Ker}({B}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))
only intersects at zero with the fixed point set
H^{G}
.
Then one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

0\in X
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
;

(ii)

there exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\mathbb{R}

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\ell(SH^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}})

and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
),

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct critical

G
-orbits different from
0
, which converge to

0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

In particular,
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times X

is a bifurcation point of (
6.1
).

By Remark
5.14
,
\ell(SH^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}})=\dim H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
(resp.
\frac{1}{2}\dim H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
)
if the Lie group
G
is equal to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{{\rm id},-{\rm id}\}
(resp.
S^{1}
).

Corollary 6.7
.

Under the assumptions of one of Corollaries
6.4
and
6.5

let
G
be a compact Lie group acting on
H
orthogonally, which induces a
C^{1}
isometric action on
X
.
Suppose that
\mathscr{L},\widehat{\mathscr{L}}
are
G
-invariant and that

A,B
and
\widehat{A},\widehat{B}
are equivariant.
Then the conclusions of Theorem
6.6
hold.

With methods in
[
2
, §7.5]

it is possible to make further generalizations for the above results.

Appendix A
Appendix: Parameterized splitting theorems by the author

Under Hypothesis
1.1
or Hypothesis
1.3

let
H=H^{+}\oplus H^{0}\oplus H^{-}
be the orthogonal decomposition
according to the positive definite, null and negative definite spaces of
B(0)
.
Denote by
P^{\ast}
the orthogonal projections onto
H^{\ast}
,
\ast=+,0,-
.

\nu:=\dim H^{0}
and
\mu:=\dim H^{-}
are called the
Morse index
and

nullity
of the critical point
0
. In particular, if
\nu=0
the critical point
0
is
said to be
nondegenerate
.
Such a critical point is isolated by
[
45
, Theorem 2.13]
(resp.
by Claim 2(i) and the arguments in Step 3 in the proof of
[
40
, Theorem 1.1]
])
for the case of Hypothesis
1.1
(resp. Hypothesis
1.3
).
We have the following parameterized Morse-Palais lemma.

Theorem A.1

(
[
45
, Theorem 2.9]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
satisfy Hypothesis
1.1
, and
let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
satisfy Hypothesis
1.2

without requirement that each
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(u)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact.
Suppose that the critical point
0
of
\mathcal{L}
is a nondegenerate.
Then there exist
\rho>0
,
\epsilon>0
, a family of open neighborhoods of
0
in

H
,
\{W_{\lambda}\,|\,|\lambda|\leq\rho\}

and a family of origin-preserving homeomorphisms,
\phi_{\lambda}:B_{H^{+}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}}(0,\epsilon)\to W_{\lambda}
,
|\lambda|\leq\rho
,
such that

(\mathcal{L}+\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}})\circ\phi_{\lambda}(u^{+}+u^{-})=\|u^{+}\|^{2}-\|u^{-}\|^{2},\quad\forall(u^{+},u^{-})\in B_{H^{+}}(0,\epsilon)\times B_{H^{-}}(0,\epsilon).

Moreover,
[-\rho,\rho]\times(B_{H^{+}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}}(0,\epsilon))\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\phi_{\lambda}(u)\in H

is continuous, and
0
is an isolated critical point of each
\mathcal{L}+\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
.

There also exist the parameterized Morse-Palais lemma around critical orbits (
[
45
, Theorem 2.21]
),
the parameterized splitting theorem around critical orbits (
[
45
, Theorem 2.22]
) and the corresponding
parameterized shifting theorem (
[
45
, Corollary 2.27]
).

Under Hypothesis
1.3
, it easily follows from the proof of
[
45
, Theorem 2.9]

and
[
41
, Remark 2.2]
that Theorem
A.1
has the following corresponding version.

Theorem A.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
satisfy Hypothesis
1.3
, and
let
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
satisfy Hypothesis
1.4

without requirement that each
\widehat{B}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact.
Suppose that the critical point
0
of
\mathcal{L}
is a nondegenerate.
Then the conclusions of Theorem
A.1
still hold.

We can also prove a more general version of this result if the condition
“
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))\neq\{0\}
” in the assumptions of the following
theorem is changed into “
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))=\{0\}
”.

Theorem A.3
.

Let
H
,
X
and
U
be as in Hypothesis
1.3
,
and
\Lambda
a topological space.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, be a continuous family of functionals
satisfying
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\lambda}(0)=0\;\forall\lambda
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, assume that
there exists a map
A_{\lambda}\in C^{1}(U^{X},X)

such that
\Lambda\times U^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\to A_{\lambda}(x)\in X
is continuous, and
that for all
x\in U\cap X
and
u,v\in X
,

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u]=(A_{\lambda}(x),u)_{H}\quad\hbox{and}\quad(DA_{\lambda}(x)[u],v)_{H}=(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H},

and that
B_{\lambda}
has a decomposition

B_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}+Q_{\lambda}
, where for each
x\in U\cap X
,

P_{\lambda}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is positive definitive and

Q_{\lambda}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact.
Let
0\in H
be a degenerate critical point of
some
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
, i.e.,
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))\neq\{0\}
.
Suppose also that
P_{\lambda}
and
Q_{\lambda}
satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

For each
h\in H
, it holds that
\|P_{\lambda}(x)h-P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)h\|\to 0

as
x\in U\cap X
approaches to
0
in
H
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
converges to
\lambda^{\ast}
.

(ii)

For some small
\delta>0
, there exist positive constants
c_{0}>0
such that

(P_{\lambda}(x)u,u)\geq c_{0}\|u\|^{2}\quad\forall u\in H,\;\forall x\in\bar{B}_{H}(0,\delta)\cap X,\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda.

(iii)

Q_{\lambda}:U\cap X\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is uniformly continuous at
0
with respect to
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

(iv)

If
\lambda\in\Lambda
converges to
\lambda^{\ast}
then

\|Q_{\lambda}(0)-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|\to 0
.

(v)

(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}},H,X,U,A_{\lambda^{\ast}},B_{\lambda^{\ast}}=P_{\lambda^{\ast}}+Q_{\lambda^{\ast}})
satisfies Hypothesis
1.3
.

Let
H^{+}_{\lambda}
,
H^{-}_{\lambda}
and
H^{0}_{\lambda}
be the positive definite, negative definite and zero spaces of

{B}_{\lambda}(0)
. Denote by
P^{0}_{\lambda}
and
P^{\pm}_{\lambda}
the orthogonal projections onto
H^{0}_{\lambda}
and
H^{\pm}_{\lambda}=H^{+}_{\lambda}\oplus H^{-}_{\lambda}
,
and by
X^{\star}_{\lambda}=X\cap H^{\star}_{\lambda}
for
\star=+,-
, and by
X^{\pm}_{\lambda}=P^{\pm}_{\lambda}(X)
.
Then there exists a neighborhood
\Lambda_{0}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\Lambda
,

\epsilon>0
, a (unique)
C^{0}
map

\psi:\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

(A.1)

which is
C^{1}
in the second variable and
satisfies
\psi(\lambda,0)=0\;\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
and

P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}A_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z))=0\quad\forall(\lambda,z)\in\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon),

(A.2)

an open neighborhood
W
of
0
in
H
and a homeomorphism

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times\left(B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\right)\to\Lambda_{0}\times W,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt({\lambda},z,u^{+}+u^{-})\mapsto({\lambda},\Phi_{{\lambda}}(z,u^{+}+u^{-}))

(A.3)

satisfying
\Phi_{{\lambda}}(0)=0
, such that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\circ\Phi_{\lambda}(z,u^{+}+u^{-})=\|u^{+}\|^{2}-\|u^{-}\|^{2}+\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}}(z+\psi({\lambda},z))

(A.4)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\forall(z,u^{+}+u^{-})\in B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times\left(B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\right).

Moreover, there also hold:
(i)

d_{z}\psi(\lambda,z)=-[P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\circ({B}_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z))|_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}})]^{-1}\circ(P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\circ({B}_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z))|_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}})).

(ii)
The functional

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}:B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to\mathbb{R},\;z\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(z+\psi({\lambda},z))

(A.5)

is of class
C^{2}
, its first-order and second-order differentials at
z\in B_{H^{0}}(0,\epsilon)
are given by

\displaystyle d\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}(z)[\zeta]=\bigl(A_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z)),\zeta\bigr)_{H}\quad\forall\zeta\in H^{0},

(A.6)

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}(0)[z,z^{\prime}]=\left(P^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\bigr[{B}_{\lambda}(0)-{B}_{\lambda}(0)(P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}{B}_{\lambda}(0)|_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}})^{-1}(P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}{B}_{\lambda}(0))\bigr]z,z^{\prime}\right)_{H},

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\forall z,z^{\prime}\in H^{0}.

(A.7)

(iii)
If a compact Lie group
G
acts on
H
orthogonally, which induces
C^{1}
isometric actions on
X
,
both
U
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
are
G
-invariant (and hence
H^{0}_{\lambda}
,
H^{\pm}_{\lambda}

are
G
-invariant subspaces), then for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
the above maps
\psi(\lambda,\cdot)
and
\Phi_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)
are

G
-equivariant, and
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
is
G
-invariant.
If for some
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Lambda
is a
C^{p}
manifold and

\Lambda\times U^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A(\lambda,x):=A_{\lambda}(x)\in X
is
C^{p}
, then so is
\psi
.

Proof.

Take
\eta>0
so small that
B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\eta)\oplus B_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\eta)\subset U^{X}
.
Since
P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\circ(B(0)|_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}})
is a Banach space isomorphism from
X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
onto itself, applying
the implicit function theorem to the map

\Lambda\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\eta)\oplus B_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\eta)\to X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}},\;(\lambda,z,x)\mapsto P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(A_{\lambda}(z+x))

near
(\lambda^{\ast},0)
we can get conclusions (
A.1
)-(
A.2
) and (i)-(ii).

By (v),
X^{\star}_{\lambda^{\ast}}=H^{\star}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
,
\star=0,-
, have finite dimensions.
Let
e_{1},\cdots,e_{m}
be an unit orthogonal basis of
H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\oplus H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.
By the proof of
[
40
, Theorem 1.1]
(or
the proof of
[
41
, Lemma 3.3]
), we have

\displaystyle|(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H}-(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u,v)_{H}|\leq\omega_{\lambda}(x)\|u\|\|v\|

(A.8)

for any
x\in U\cap X
,
u\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\oplus H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
and
v\in H
, where

\omega_{\lambda}(x)=\left(\sum^{m}_{i=1}\|P_{\lambda}(x)e_{i}-P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)e_{i}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\sqrt{m}\|Q_{\lambda}(x)-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|.

Clearly, assumptions (i), (iii) and (iv) imply that
\omega_{\lambda}(x)\to 0

as
x\in U\cap X
approaches to
0
in
H
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
converges to
\lambda^{\ast}
.
It is clear that (
A.8
) leads to

\displaystyle|(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H}|\leq\omega_{\lambda}(x)\|u\|\|v\|,\quad\forall u\in H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}},\;\forall v\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\oplus H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

(A.9)

for any
x\in U\cap X
.
Moreover, there exists
a_{0}>0
such that
(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u,u)_{H}\geq 2a_{0}\|u\|^{2}\;\forall u\in H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.
Take a neighborhood of
0\in H
,
V\subset U
, and shrink
\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda
, such that

\omega_{\lambda}(x)<a_{0}
for all
x\in V\cap X
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
.
It follows from this and (
A.8
) that

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda}(x)v,v)_{H}\leq-2a_{0}\|v\|^{2}+\omega_{\lambda}(x)\|v\|^{2}\leq-a_{0}\|v\|^{2}\quad\forall v\in H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

(A.10)

for all
x\in V\cap X
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
. As in the proof of
[
41
, Lemma 3.4]
(i), (see below),
by shrinking
V
and
\Lambda_{0}
we can get
a_{1}>0
such that
for all
x\in V\cap X
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda}(x)u,u)_{H}\geq a_{1}\|u\|^{2}\quad\forall u\in H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}.

(A.11)

Since
\psi(\lambda,0)=0
, we can choose
\epsilon\in(0,\eta)
such that

z+\psi(\lambda,z)+u^{+}+u^{-}\in V
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,

z\in\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
and
u^{\star}\in\bar{B}_{H^{\star}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
,
\star=+,-
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
, we define a functional

\displaystyle{\bf F}_{\lambda}:B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to\mathbb{R},(z,u)\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(z+\psi({\lambda},z)+u)-\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}}(z+\psi({\lambda},z)).

Then following the proof ideas of
[
41
, Lemma 3.5]
and shrinking
\Lambda_{0}
and
\epsilon>0

(if necessary) we can obtain positive constants
\mathfrak{a}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
such that

\displaystyle(D_{2}{\bf F}_{{\lambda}}(z,u^{+}+u^{-}_{2})-D_{2}{\bf F}_{{\lambda}}(z,u^{+}+u^{-}_{1}))[u^{-}_{2}-u^{-}_{1}]\leq-\mathfrak{a}_{1}\|u^{-}_{2}-u^{-}_{1}\|^{2},

\displaystyle D_{2}{\bf F}_{{\lambda}}(z,u^{+}+u^{-})[u^{+}-u^{-}]\geq\mathfrak{a}_{2}(\|u^{+}\|^{2}+\|u^{-}\|^{2})

for all
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,

z\in B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
and
u^{+}\in B_{X^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
,
u^{-}\in B_{X^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
.
Using
[
41
, Theorem A.2]
leads to the desired results.

Finally, for completeness let us prove (
A.11
) by contradiction.

Then there exist
sequences
(x_{n})\subset V\cap X
with
\|x_{n}\|\to 0
,
(\lambda_{n})\subset\Lambda
with
\lambda_{n}\to\lambda^{\ast}

and

(u_{n})\subset SH^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
, such that

(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}<1/n
for any
n=1,2,\cdots
.
Passing a subsequence, we can assume that

(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}\to\beta\leq 0\;\hbox{as}\;n\to\infty,

(A.12)

and that
u_{n}\rightharpoonup u_{0}
in
H
. We claim:
u_{0}\neq\theta
. In fact, by (ii) we have

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}+(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}

(A.13)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{0}+(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}\quad\forall n>n_{0}.

Moreover, a direct computation gives

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\quad|(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

(A.14)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!=|((Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))u_{n},u_{n})_{H}+(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{n},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n})_{H}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\leq\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|\cdot\|u_{n}\|^{2}+\|Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{n}-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0}\|\cdot\|u_{n}\|

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\leq\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|+\|Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{n}-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0}\|+|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|.

Since
u_{n}\rightharpoonup u_{0}
in
H
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|=0
. We have also

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{n}-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0}\|=0

(A.15)

by the compactness of
Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)
, and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda_{n}}(0)\|+\lim_{n\to\infty}\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(0)-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|=0

(A.16)

by the conditions (iii)-(iv).
Hence (
A.14
)-(
A.16
) give

\lim_{n\to\infty}(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}=(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}.

(A.17)

This and (
A.12
)-(
A.13
) yield

0\geq\beta=\lim_{n\to\infty}(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}\geq c_{0}+(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H},

which implies
u_{0}\neq\theta
. Note that
u_{0}
also sits in
H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.

As above, using (
A.16
) we derive

\displaystyle|(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

(A.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n})_{H}|

\displaystyle+|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|\cdot\|u_{0}\|+|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|\to 0.

Note that

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}+(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{0}\|u_{n}-u_{0}\|^{2}+(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}-2(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}+(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}.

It follows from this and (
A.17
)-(
A.18
) that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}=0.

(A.19)

Note that
u_{n}\rightharpoonup u_{0}
implies that
(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}\to 0
. By (D2) and (
A.18
) we get

\displaystyle|(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}+(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}

\displaystyle-(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|(P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle+|(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|(P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n})_{H}|+|(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\quad+|(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|P_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0}-P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0}\|+|(P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}|

\displaystyle\quad+|(Q_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}-(Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}|\to 0.

Similarly, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{0})_{H}=(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}
.
From these, (
A.12
) and (
A
) it follows that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})(u_{n}-u_{0}),u_{n}-u_{0})_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}[(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}-2(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{n})_{H}+(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{0},u_{0})_{H}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}(B_{\lambda_{n}}(x_{n})u_{n},u_{n})_{H}-(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}=\beta-(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}.

Namely,
(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u_{0},u_{0})_{H}\leq\beta\leq 0
. It contradicts to
the fact that
(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)u,u)_{H}\geq 2a_{0}\|u\|^{2}\;\forall u\in H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

because
u_{0}\in H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\setminus\{0\}
.

As usual, conclusions in (iii) can be easily obtained from the above proof.
∎

Remark A.4
.

(i)
As in the proof of
[
45
, Claim 2.17]
we may show:
if
0\in H
is a nondegenerate critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
,
i.e.,
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda}(0))=\{0\}
,
then
0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is such a critical point of
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
too.

(ii)
Every critical point
z
of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
in

B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
yields a critical point
z+\psi({\lambda},z)

of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
, and
z+\psi({\lambda},z)
sits in
X
.
Moreover, if
z\to 0
in
H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
, then
z+\psi({\lambda},z)\to 0
in
X
.
Conversely, every critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{X}
near
0\in X

has the form
z+\psi({\lambda},z)
, where
z\in B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
is a
critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
.
Clearly, if
0\in H
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
,
then
0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is also an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
.
Conversely, it might not be true, which is different from
[
45
, Theorem 2.2]
.

Let
\nu_{\lambda}
and
\mu_{\lambda}
denote the nullity and Morse index
of the functional
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
at
0
. They are finite numbers and are
equal to
\dim H_{\lambda}^{0}
and
\dim H^{-}_{\lambda}
(that is, the nullity and Morse index
of the quadratic form
({B}_{\lambda}u,u)
on
H
), respectively.
As in
[
41
, Corollary 2.6]
we have

Corollary A.5

(Shifting)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
A.3
, suppose
for some
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]
that
0\in H
is an isolated
critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}}
(thus
0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
is that of
\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}}^{\circ}
). Then

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}},0;{\bf K})=C_{q-\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{{\lambda}},0;{\bf K}),\quad\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

Corollary A.6
.

Under Hypothesis
1.5
the conclusions of Theorem
A.3

hold with family
\{\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{L}-\lambda\widehat{\mathcal{L}}\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda=\mathbb{R}\}
.

Remark A.7
.

If Hypothesis
1.5
in Corollary
A.6
is replaced by
Hypothesis
1.6
, that is, we only assume that
A:U^{X}\to X
is
Gâteaux differentiable, and strictly Fréchet differentiable at
0\in U^{X}
,
then we can still prove Corollary
A.6
with weaker conclusions
that
\psi
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
are
C^{1-0}
and
C^{2-0}
, respectively,
and that all
\psi(\lambda,\cdot)
and
d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
are strictly Fréchet differentiable
at
0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.

Remark A.8
.

From the proofs of
[
13
, Theorem 5.1.13]
and
[
49
, Theorem 8.3]
it is easily seen that
the classical splitting lemma for
C^{2}
functionals has also the parameterized version as Theorem
A.3
.
Let
U\subset H
be as in Theorem
A.3
,
\Lambda
a topological space,
and let
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\in C^{2}(U,\mathbb{R})
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, be a family of functionals
satisfying
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\lambda}(0)=0\;\forall\lambda
, and be such that

\Lambda\times U\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u)\in H
is continuous.
For some
\lambda^{\ast}\in\Lambda
, assume that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)
is a Fredholm operator with nontrivial kernel.
Then the conclusions of Theorem
A.3
hold as long as we take
X=H
,
A_{\lambda}=\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
and

B_{\lambda}=\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}
, and replace the paragraph
“ an open neighborhood
W
of
0
in
H
and a homeomorphism ……
satisfying
\Phi_{{\lambda}}(0)=0
” by
“ a
C^{0}
map

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to H,\quad({\lambda},u)\mapsto\Phi_{{\lambda}}(u)

such that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
the map

\Phi_{\lambda}
is an origin-preserving homeomorphism from

B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
onto
an open neighborhood
W_{\lambda}
of
0
in
H
”.

If the kernel of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)
is trivial,
we have a corresponding parameterized Morse-Palais lemma.

Moreover, there also exist corresponding corollaries of Theorem
A.2
,
A.3
as above.

As noted in
[
41
, Remark 2.4]
, these can directly follow from
Theorem
A.2
,
A.3
and their corollaries if
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)

has also a finite dimensional negative definite space.

Appendix B
Appendix: Parameterized Bobylev-Burman splitting lemmas

We here give parameterized versions of splitting lemmas in
[
8
]

in consistent notations with those of this paper.
Let
H
be a Hilbert space with inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{H}

and the induced norm
\|\cdot\|
,
X
a Banach space with
norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
, such that

(S)

X\subset H
is dense in
H
and
the inclusion
X\hookrightarrow H
is continuous.

Suppose that a
C^{2}
functional
\mathscr{L}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathbb{R}

satisfies for some constant
M>0
:

(a)

|d\mathscr{L}(x)[u]|\leq M\|u\|,\;\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
\forall u\in X
.

(b)

|d^{2}\mathscr{L}(x)[u,v]|\leq M\|u\|\cdot\|v\|,\;\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
\forall u,v\in X
.

Then there is a map
B:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
such that

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathscr{L}(x)[u,v]=(B(x)u,v)_{H}

(B.1)

for all
x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
and
u,v\in X
.
Suppose also:

(c)

There is a uniformly continuously differentiable map
A:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X
such that

d\mathscr{L}(x)[u]=(A(x),u)_{H}
for all
x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
and for all
u\in X
.
(Hence for any
x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,
(
B.1
) implies that
B(x)(X)\subset X

and
B(x)y=dA(x)[y]\;\forall y\in X
. As an element in
\mathscr{L}(X)
we write

B(x)|_{X}=dA(x)
without confusions.)

(d)

B(\cdot)|_{X}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}(X)
is continuously differentiable,
and also uniformly differentiable in a neighborhood of the line segment

[0,1]u:=\{tu\,|\,0\leq t\leq 1\}
for each
u\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
. (The latter is satisfied
if
A
is
C^{3}
!)

(e)

B:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is uniformly continuous.

A functional
\mathscr{L}
satisfying the above conditions (a)-(e) is called
(B_{X}(0,\delta),H)
-regular
in
[
8
]
.

Remark B.1
.

The second condition in (d) did not appear in
[
8
]
. We consider that it is needed for the proof of

C^{1}
-smoothness of the operator

B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni u\mapsto\mathcal{B}(u):=\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)A^{\prime}(tu)dt=\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)B(tu)|_{X}dt\in\mathscr{L}(X),

which corresponds to the operator
A(\cdot):B\to S(E)
in
[
8
, Lemma 1.2]
. In fact,
(c) implies that
B(\cdot)|_{X}=dA:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}(X)
is
uniformly continuous and hence that
\mathcal{B}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}(X)
is continuous.
(Actually, the latter claim may follow from the differentiability of the map
A:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X

and the first condition in (d). Indeed, the former can still lead to
B(x)|_{X}=dA(x)
for any

x\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
. Then the first condition in (d) implies that
A
is
C^{2}
.
Therefore for a fixed
u_{0}\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
there are
M>0
and a convex neighborhood
\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})
of the compact
subset
[0,1]u_{0}\subset B_{X}(0,\delta)
such that
\|A^{\prime\prime}(x)\|_{\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))}\leq M
for all
x\in\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})
.
Take
\rho>0
so small that
B_{X}(u_{0},\rho)\subset B_{X}(0,\delta)
and
[0,1]u\subset\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})

for all
u\in B_{X}(u_{0},\rho)
. It follows from the mean value theorem that for
u_{i}\in B_{X}(u_{0},\rho)
,
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}(u_{2})-\mathcal{B}(u_{1})\|_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\leq\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)\|A^{\prime}(tu_{2})-A^{\prime}(tu_{1})\|_{\mathscr{L}(X)}dt\leq M\|u_{2}-u_{1}\|_{X}.

That is,
\mathcal{B}
is locally Lipschitz.)

By the differential method with parameter integral we can only derive from the first assumption in (d) that

\mathcal{B}
has a Gâteaux derivative at
u\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,

D\mathcal{B}(u)=\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)tA^{\prime\prime}(tu)dt\in\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X)).

When
\dim X<\infty
, for any
0<\epsilon<\delta
, since the closure of
B_{X}(0,\delta-\epsilon)

is a compact subset contained in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
,

A^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly continuous on
Cl(B_{X}(0,\delta-\epsilon))
,
it follows that
u\mapsto D\mathcal{B}(u)
is continuous and so
\mathcal{B}
is
C^{1}
.

If
\dim X=\infty
, without the second condition in (d) it is difficult for us to prove that

D\mathcal{B}
is continuous at
u
except
u=0\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
.
For
u,u_{0}\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
, since

\displaystyle\|D\mathcal{B}(u)-D\mathcal{B}(u_{0})\|_{\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))}\leq\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)t\|A^{\prime\prime}(tu)-A^{\prime\prime}(tu_{0})\|_{\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))}dt

and the second condition in (d) implies that
A^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly continuous in a neighborhood of the line segment

[0,1]u_{0}
, we conclude that
D\mathcal{B}(u)\to D\mathcal{B}(u_{0})
as
u\to u_{0}
.

When
A
is
C^{3}
,
A^{\prime\prime\prime}
is bounded in a convex neighborhood of the line segment

[0,1]u_{0}
. It follows from the mean value theorem that

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|A^{\prime\prime}(tu)-A^{\prime\prime}(tu_{0})\|_{\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))}\to 0\quad\hbox{as $u\to u_{0}$.}

Hence
\mathcal{B}
is
C^{1}
.

In summary,
all assumptions of differentiability on
A
and
B
in (c)-(d) can be replaced by one of the following two conditions:

(i)

A
is
C^{3}
.

(ii)

A
is
C^{2}
and

A^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly continuous in some neighborhood of the line segment

[0,1]u:=\{tu\,|\,0\leq t\leq 1\}
for each
u\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
.

The above arguments suggest that proofs as in

[
6
, Theorem 6.5.4]
,
[
71
, Theorem 1.4]
,
[
23
, page 436, Theorem 1]
and

[
8
, Theorem 1.1]
are defective.

Finally, the condition (e) seems not to be needed, see proofs of Theorems
B.3
,
B.6
,
B.8
.

In this paper,
by the spectrum of a linear operator on a real Banach space
we always mean one of its natural complex linear extension on the complexification of
the real Banach space
(
[
21
, page 14]
). Similarly, if others cannot be clearly explained in the real world,
we consider their complexification and then take invariant parts under complex adjoint on the complexification
spaces.

Let
\mathscr{S}(X):=\{L\in\mathscr{L}(X)\,|\,(Lu,v)_{H}=(u,Lv)_{H}\;\forall u,v\in X\}
.
Recall the following Riesz lemma (cf.
[
8
, Lemma 1.3]
and
[
21
, page 19]
,
[
70
, page 54, Lemma 1]
and

[
70
, page 59, Propositions 2,3]
).

Lemma B.2
.

Under the assumption
(S)
, let
D\in\mathscr{S}(X)
and its spectrum have a decomposition

\sigma(D)=\{0\}\cup\sigma_{+}(D)\cup\sigma_{-}(D),

where
\sigma_{+}(D)
and
\sigma_{-}(D)
are closed subsets of
\sigma(D)
contained in
the interiors of the left and right half planes, respectively.
Then the space
X
can be decomposed into a direct sum of
spaces
X_{0}
,
X_{+}
and
X_{-}
, which are closed in
X
, invariant with respect to
D
, orthogonal in
H
, and

\sigma(D_{\ast})=\sigma_{\ast}(D)
for
D_{\ast}=D|_{X_{\ast}}
,
\ast=+,-
. The projections
P_{0}
,
P_{+}
and
P_{-}
,
corresponding to this decomposition, are symmetric operators with respect to the inner product in
H
, and
satisfy

(P_{\ast})^{2}=P_{\ast},\quad\ast=0,+,-,\quad P_{0}+P_{+}+P_{-}=id_{X},

and any two of three projections have zero compositions,
and moreover
P_{\ast}
,
\ast=+,-,0
, are expressible as a limit of power series in
D
.
In addition, square roots
S_{-}
and
S_{+}
to
-D_{-}
and
D_{+}

may be defined with the functional calculus, and are expressible in power
series in
D_{-}
and
D_{+}
, and also symmetric with respect to the inner product in
H
.

For the spectrum
\sigma(B(0)|_{X})
of the operator
B(0)|_{X}\in\mathscr{L}(X)
in (d) suppose:

\sigma(B(0)|_{X})\setminus\{0\}
is bounded away from the imaginary axis.

By Lemma
B.2
, corresponding to the spectral sets
\{0\}
,

\sigma_{+}(B(0)|_{X})
and
\sigma_{-}(B(0)|_{X})

we have a direct sum decomposition of Banach spaces,

X=X_{0}\oplus X_{+}\oplus X_{-}
. Denote by
P_{0}
,
P_{+}
and
P_{-}

the projections corresponding to the decomposition.
When
\sigma(B(0)|_{X})
does not intersect the imaginary axis,
namely the operator
B(0)|_{X}\in\mathscr{L}(X)
is
hyperbolic
(
[
72
]
),
(in particular,
\dim X_{0}=0
),
we say that the critical point
0
is
nondegenerate
.
(Such nondegenerate critical points are isolated by Morse lemma
[
8
, Theorem 1.1]
.)
Call
\nu:=\dim X_{0}
and
\mu:=\dim X_{-}

the
nullity
and the
Morse index
of
0
, respectively.
(Using Lemma
B.2
we may prove
that
\dim X_{-}
is the supremum of the dimensions of the vector subspaces
of
X
on which
(B(0)u,u)_{H}
is negative definite).
The following is a parametrized version of
[
8
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Theorem B.3
.

Let
\Lambda
be a metric space, and

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathbb{R}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
a family of
C^{2}
-functionals satisfying the conditions (a)-(b) and
d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(0)=0\;\forall\lambda
.
Let
B_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
be defined by

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u,v]=(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H},\quad\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta),\;\forall u,v\in X.

Suppose:

(c’)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
there is a
C^{2}
map
A_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X
such that

d\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u]=(A_{\lambda}(x),u)_{H},\quad\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\delta),\quad\forall u\in X.

(Therefore each
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}
is
C^{3}
.)

(d’)

For each point
(\lambda_{0},u_{0})\in\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)
there exist
neighborhoods
\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})
of
\lambda_{0}
in
\Lambda
, and
\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})
of

[0,1]u_{0}
in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})\times\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto dA_{\lambda}(u)\in\mathscr{L}(X)\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})\times\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto d^{2}A_{\lambda}(u)\in\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))

are uniform continuous.

(h)

For some
\lambda^{\ast}\in\Lambda
the operator
B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X}\in\mathscr{L}(X)

is hyperbolic (i.e.,
0
is a nondegenerate critical point of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
).

Then there exist a neighborhood
\Lambda_{0}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\Lambda
,

\epsilon_{0}\in(0,\delta)
, and a family of origin-preserving
C^{1}
maps

\varphi_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\epsilon_{0})\to X
,
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
, such that
both
(\lambda,u)\mapsto\varphi_{\lambda}(u)
and
(\lambda,u)\mapsto\varphi^{\prime}_{\lambda}(u)
are of class
C^{0}
,
and that each
\varphi_{\lambda}
is a
C^{1}
diffeomorphism
from
B_{X}(0,\epsilon_{0})
onto an open neighborhood of zero in
X
containing
B_{X}(0,\epsilon_{0}/4)

and satisfies

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}\circ\varphi_{\lambda}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)x,x)_{H}+\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(0),\quad\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon_{0}),\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}.

(B.2)

Moreover, if
\Lambda
is a nonempty open subset of a Banach space
Z
,
then
(\lambda,u)\mapsto\varphi_{\lambda}(u)
is of class
C^{1}
provided that (d’) is replaced by the following three conditions:

(f1)

\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto A(\lambda,u):=A_{\lambda}(u)\in X

and

\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto D_{2}A(\lambda,u)\in\mathscr{L}(X)

are
C^{1}
. Here
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are differentials of
A
with respect to
\lambda
and
u
, respectively.

(f2)

Either
\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto D^{2}_{2}A(\lambda,u)\in\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))

is
C^{1}
, or for each point
(\lambda_{0},u_{0})\in\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)
there exist
neighborhoods
\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})
of
\lambda_{0}
in
\Lambda
, and
\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})
of

[0,1]u_{0}
in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
such that

\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})\times\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto D^{2}_{2}A(\lambda,u)\in\mathscr{L}(X,\mathscr{L}(X))

is uniform continuous.

(f3)

Either
\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto D_{1}D_{2}A(\lambda,u)\in\mathscr{L}(Z,\mathscr{L}(X))

is
C^{1}
, or for each point
(\lambda_{0},u_{0})\in\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)
there exist
neighborhoods
\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})
of
\lambda_{0}
in
\Lambda
, and
\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})
of

[0,1]u_{0}
in
B_{X}(0,\delta)
such that

\mathscr{N}(\lambda_{0})\times\mathscr{N}([0,1]u_{0})\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto D_{1}D_{2}A(\lambda,u)\in\mathscr{L}(Z,\mathscr{L}(X))

is uniform continuous.

(It is clear that (d’) and these three conditions are satisfied if

\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto A(\lambda,u)\in X

is
C^{3}
.
Remark
B.5
(iii) also give other substitution conditions.)

Clearly, (
B.2
) implies that the nondegenerate critical point
0
of
\mathscr{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

is isolated.

Let
X=X^{\lambda^{\ast}}_{+}\oplus X_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}
be a direct sum decomposition of Banach spaces
corresponding to the spectral sets
\sigma_{+}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X})

and
\sigma_{-}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X})
, and
P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}
and
P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}

denote the projections corresponding to the decomposition.
Let
S^{-}
and
S^{+}
be square roots to
-B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X_{-}}
and
B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)|_{X_{+}}
,
respectively. Then

\psi:X\to X,\;x\mapsto S_{+}P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x+S_{-}P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x,

is a Banach space isomorphism, and

\displaystyle(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)x,x)_{H}=\|S_{+}P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}-\|S_{-}P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}S_{+}P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}-\|P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}S_{-}P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}\psi(x)\|_{H}^{2}-\|P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}\psi(x)\|_{H}^{2}

Take
\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
such that
\psi^{-1}(B_{X}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}))\subset B_{X}(0,\epsilon_{0})
.
By (
B.2
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}\circ(\varphi_{\lambda}\circ\psi^{-1})(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|P_{+}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}-\|P_{-}^{\lambda^{\ast}}x\|_{H}^{2}+\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(0),

(B.3)

\displaystyle\quad\forall x\in B_{X}(0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}),\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}.

If
\nu:=\min\{\epsilon_{0},\inf\{\|x\|\,|\,\|\psi(x)\|_{X}=\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}\}\}
then
B_{X}(0,\nu)\subset\psi^{-1}(B(0,\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}))

and so

B_{X}(0,\nu/4)\subset\varphi_{\lambda}(B_{X}(0,\nu))\subset(\varphi_{\lambda}\circ\psi^{-1})(B(0,\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}))\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}

by the final claim in the following Lemma
B.4
.

For the proofs of Theorems
B.3
,
B.8
we need the following version of inverse function theorems,
which may be derived from
[
51
, Lemma A.3.2]
directly.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
X
and
Y
be Banach spaces,
B_{X}(x_{0},r)\subset X

an open ball with radius
r
and center
x_{0}
,
and
f\in C^{1}(B_{X}(x_{0},r),Y)
. Suppose that
df(x_{0})\in\mathscr{L}(X,Y)
is invertible
and that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that

\|[df(x_{0})]^{-1}\|\leq\alpha\quad\hbox{and}\quad\|df(x)-df(x_{0})\|\leq\frac{1}{2\alpha}\;\forall x\in B_{X}(x_{0},r).

Then
f(B_{X}(x_{0},r))\subset Y
is an open subset containing
B_{Y}(f(x_{0}),\frac{r}{2\alpha})

and
f
is a
C^{1}
diffeomorphism from
B_{X}(x_{0},r)
to
f(B_{X}(x_{0},r))
.
Moreover, if
f
is
C^{k}
for some
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
then so is
f^{-1}:f(B_{X}(x_{0},r))\to B_{X}(x_{0},r)
.

(
Clearly,
r
in the conclusions can be replaced by any
r_{0}\in(0,r]
.
)

For the sake of clarity we also give the proof of Theorem
B.3

though it is a slight modification of that of
[
8
, Theorem 1.1]
.
In a note after the proof we shall show that (d’) can be slightly weakened.

Proof of Theorem
B.3
.

Since
A_{\lambda}:B_{X}(0,\delta)\to X
is
C^{2}
,
for any
u\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(u):=\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)A^{\prime}_{\lambda}(tu)dt=\int^{1}_{0}(1-t)B_{\lambda}(tu)|_{X}dt

(B.4)

is a well-defined operator

\mathscr{L}(X)
and symmetric with respect to
(\cdot,\cdot)_{H}
.
It was proved in
[
8
, Lemma 1.2]
that

\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}(u)=(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(u)u,u)_{H}
for all
u\in B_{X}(0,\delta)
.
By Lemma
B.14
,

\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(u)\in\mathscr{L}(X)
is continuous,
C^{1}
in
u
, and

\Lambda\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\ni(\lambda,u)\mapsto\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\lambda}(u)\in\mathscr{L}(X)
is also continuous.
If the conditions in the “Moreover” part hold the map
